**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

## FEEDBACK OUTPUT
Let’s go step by step.  

---

### 1. Compilation errors in optimized code  
The optimized code compiles without errors (no syntax issues).  

---

### 2. Semantic comparison  

**Source code logic** (for `i = 1` to `LEN_1D-1`):  
```
a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]
```
Each iteration uses the **updated** `a[i-1]` from the previous iteration in the same `nl` loop.  

**Optimized code logic**:  
```c
float prev_a = a[0];
for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
    float temp = prev_a + s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
    prev_a = a[i];   // <-- reads old a[i] before it’s overwritten
    a[i] = temp;
}
```
Let’s test with a small example:  

**Initial**:  
`a = [1, 2, 3]`, `b = [0, 10, 20]`, `c = [0, 100, 200]`, `d = [0, 1000, 2000]`, `s1 = 1`, `s2 = 2`, `s_sum = 3`.  

**Source code iteration** (`i=1`):  
`a[1] = 1 + 3 + 10 + 100 + 1000 = 1114` → `a = [1, 1114, 3]`  

`i=2`:  
`a[2] = 1114 + 3 + 20 + 200 + 2000 = 3337` → `a = [1, 1114, 3337]`  

**Optimized code iteration**:  
`prev_a = 1`  
`i=1`: `temp = 1 + 3 + 10 + 100 + 1000 = 1114`, `prev_a = a[1] = 2`, `a[1] = 1114` → `a = [1, 1114, 3]`  

`i=2`: `temp = prev_a + 3 + 20 + 200 + 2000 = 2 + 2223 = 2225`, `prev_a = a[2] = 3`, `a[2] = 2225` → `a = [1, 1114, 2225]`  

Result differs: `3337` vs `2225`.  

**Why**: In the optimized code, `prev_a = a[i]` reads the **old** `a[i]` before it’s overwritten, but for the next iteration, we should use the **new** `a[i]` (which is `temp`), not the old one.  

Thus, the optimized code’s semantics are **incorrect** — it propagates the wrong `a[i-1]` value.  

---

**Corrected version** of the optimized code would be:  
```c
float prev_a = a[0];
for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
    prev_a = prev_a + s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
    a[i] = prev_a;
}
```
This matches the source code’s recurrence.  

---

Since the given optimized code has wrong semantics, we stop here.  

**FAIL**

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

    float s_sum = s1 + s2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        // Precompute independent terms
        float* tmp = (float*)__builtin_alloca(LEN_1D * sizeof(float));
        
        // Vectorizable loop: compute all independent components
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            tmp[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
        }
        
        // Sequential prefix sum (keeps dependency but cleaner)
        float prev_a = a[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            prev_a = prev_a + tmp[i];
            a[i] = prev_a;
        }
    }
}

## FEEDBACK OUTPUT
**Analysis Report**

---

### **1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code**
The optimized code uses `__builtin_alloca(LEN_1D * sizeof(float))` inside the outer loop.  
- This is a **runtime stack allocation** that depends on `LEN_1D`.  
- If `LEN_1D` is large (e.g., > a few thousand), this can cause **stack overflow** at runtime.  
- While it may compile without syntax errors, it introduces a **serious correctness risk** that can lead to crashes.  
- This is a **semantic error** in terms of reliability and safety, even if not a compile-time error.

**Result:** FAIL due to unsafe stack allocation.

---

### **2. Semantic Comparison with Simple Test Cases**
Even ignoring the stack issue, the optimized code changes the semantics.

**Original loop:**
```c
a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
```
This is a **prefix sum** of `(s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i])` starting from `a[0]`.

**Optimized code:**
```c
tmp[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
prev_a = prev_a + tmp[i];
a[i] = prev_a;
```
This is mathematically equivalent **if** `prev_a` starts as `a[0]` and `tmp[i]` is computed correctly.

But there is a **critical bug**:  
In the original, `a[0]` is never updated in the inner loop.  
In the optimized version, `prev_a` starts as `a[0]` in **each outer iteration**, but `a[0]` may have been changed in previous outer iterations.  
However, the optimized code **reloads** `prev_a = a[0]` at the start of each outer iteration, which matches the original because the original’s first iteration `i=1` uses `a[0]` from the previous outer iteration’s final `a[0]`.  
Actually, `a[0]` is **never written** in the loop (since `i` starts at 1), so `a[0]` stays constant across all outer iterations.  
Thus, the optimized version’s `prev_a = a[0]` resets to the original `a[0]` each outer iteration, breaking the dependency across outer iterations.

**Example:**  
Let `iterations/5 = 2`, `LEN_1D = 3`, `s1=1`, `s2=2`, `a=[10,0,0]`, `b=[0,1,1]`, `c=[0,1,1]`, `d=[0,1,1]`.  
Original:  
- Outer 1:  
  i=1: a[1] = 10 + 3 + 1+1+1 = 16  
  i=2: a[2] = 16 + 3 + 1+1+1 = 22  
- Outer 2:  
  i=1: a[1] = 10 + 3 + 1+1+1 = 16 (again, because a[0] unchanged)  
  i=2: a[2] = 16 + 3 + 1+1+1 = 22  

Optimized:  
- Outer 1: tmp[1]=3+1+1+1=6, tmp[2]=6, prev_a=10 → a[1]=16, a[2]=22  
- Outer 2: **prev_a reset to a[0]=10** → same result.  

Wait — this matches? Let’s check carefully:  
In original, `a[i-1]` in outer iteration 2 uses `a[1]` from outer iteration 1, not `a[0]`.  
Yes — because `i=1` in outer iteration 2:  
`a[1] = a[0] + s1+s2 + b[1]+c[1]+d[1]`  
`a[0]` is still original 10, so indeed same as outer iteration 1’s `a[1]`.  
So actually, the original loop’s result **does not depend on previous outer iteration’s a[1..]**, only on `a[0]` (constant).  

Thus, the optimized version’s resetting `prev_a` to `a[0]` is **correct** for this specific loop.  
But there’s still a problem: the original uses `a[i-1]` from **same outer iteration**, not from previous outer iteration, because `a[i-1]` was updated earlier in same outer iteration.  
In optimized version, the `prev_a` accumulation within one outer iteration is correct.  
So mathematically, the optimized version matches the original **if** `a[0]` is unchanged.

But the stack allocation issue remains a **functional failure** for large LEN_1D.

---

### **3. Vectorization Analysis**
Compiler feedback:
- First loop (`tmp[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]`) is vectorized.
- Second loop (`prev_a = prev_a + tmp[i]`) is **not vectorized** due to loop-carried dependency (prefix sum).

This is expected — the prefix sum is inherently sequential.  
The optimization attempted to split the loop into a vectorizable part and a sequential part, which is good.  
But the stack allocation is **not safe** and should be replaced with heap or static allocation if allowed, or better, avoided.

---

### **4. Conclusion**
- **Compilation/Correctness**: FAIL due to unsafe `alloca` inside loop.
- **Semantics**: Mathematically correct for given formula but unsafe memory practice invalidates it.
- **Vectorization**: Partially vectorized, but sequential part remains (unavoidable).

**Final:** FAIL — due to stack overflow risk in optimized code.  
Fix: Remove `alloca`, use a stack-array of fixed safe size or heap allocation, but better to just compute `s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]` on the fly in the sequential loop to avoid extra memory.

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

```c
void s242(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a,float* b,float* c,float* d)
{
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {

    }
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

void s242_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d) {
    float s_sum = s1 + s2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        // Precompute independent terms without dynamic allocation
        float prev_a = a[0];
        
        // Loop distribution with instruction reordering
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            // Compute independent component
            float independent_term = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
            // Sequential prefix sum (keeps dependency)
            prev_a = prev_a + independent_term;
            a[i] = prev_a;
        }
    }
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

Source code: 
```c
void s242(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a,float* b,float* c,float* d)
{
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
        }
    }
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

void s242_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d) {
    float s_sum = s1 + s2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        // Precompute independent terms in a vectorizable loop
        float* tmp = (float*)__builtin_alloca(LEN_1D * sizeof(float));
        tmp[0] = 0.0f; // Not used, but keeps indexing consistent
        
        // Vectorizable: no loop-carried dependency
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            tmp[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
        }
        
        // Sequential prefix sum (keeps dependency)
        float prev_a = a[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            prev_a = prev_a + tmp[i];
            a[i] = prev_a;
        }
    }
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

void s242_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d) {
    float s_sum = s1 + s2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        // Store original a[0] value for correct dependency chain
        float a0_original = a[0];
        
        // Vectorizable loop: compute independent terms
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            a[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];

        // Sequential prefix sum using the computed terms
        float prev = a0_original;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            prev = prev + a[i];
            a[i] = prev;
        }
    }
}

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

## **2. Semantic comparison**

**Source code recurrence:**
```
a[i] = a[i-1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]
```
Let `K[i] = s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]`.

Then:
```
a[1] = a[0] + K[1]
a[2] = a[1] + K[2] = a[0] + K[1] + K[2]
...
a[i] = a[0] + sum_{j=1..i} K[j]
```

**Optimized code:**
First loop:
```
a[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i] = K[i]   (for i >= 1)
```
But `a[0]` is saved as `a0_original`.

Second loop:
```
prev = a0_original
for i = 1..LEN_1D-1:
    prev = prev + a[i]   // a[i] currently holds K[i]
    a[i] = prev
```
This yields:
```
a[1] = a0_original + K[1]
a[2] = a0_original + K[1] + K[2]
...
```
That matches the source recurrence exactly, because `K[i]` is computed from `s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]` and `a0_original = a[0]`.

So mathematically, the optimized code is **semantically equivalent**.

Source code: 
```c
void s242(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a,float* b,float* c,float* d)
{
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
        }
    }
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

void s242_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d) {
    float s_sum = s1 + s2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        // Store original a[0] for correct recurrence start
        float a0_original = a[0];
        
        // Vectorizable loop: compute independent terms
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            a[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
        }
        
        // Sequential prefix sum using computed terms
        float prev = a0_original;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            prev = prev + a[i];
            a[i] = prev;
        }
    }
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

```c
{

        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];

}
```

Optimized code:
```c

void s242_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d) {
    float s_sum = s1 + s2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {
        float prev = a[0];
        
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            float temp = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
            a[i] = prev + temp;
            prev = a[i];
        }
    }
}
```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

## **1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code**

The optimized code uses `__builtin_alloca(LEN_1D * sizeof(float))` inside the outer loop.  
If `LEN_1D` is large (e.g., 100000), this will allocate a large array on the stack **each iteration**, risking stack overflow.  
However, this is not a compilation error — it compiles, but is dangerous.  
Since there’s no compilation error reported, we proceed.

## **2. Semantic Comparison**

The original recurrence is:

```
a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
```

The optimized version does:

1. Precompute `temp[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]` for `i = 1..LEN_1D-1`.
2. Then compute `a[i] = prev + temp[i]` with `prev` updated each step.

`i=2`: `a[2] = 16 + 1 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 2 = 25`

Optimized:  
`s_sum = 3`.  
`temp[1] = 3 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 6`, `temp[2] = 3 + 2 + 2 + 2 = 9`.  
`prev = a[0] = 10`.  
`i=1`: `a[1] = 10 + 6 = 16`, `prev = 16`.  
`i=2`: `a[2] = 16 + 9 = 25`.

**Preservation Requirements**:

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```

    ```c
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:

     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:

     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:

     ```c
     // Original

     ```

   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

     ```c
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**

   ```c
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)

   ```

   - No overlapping writes between split segments

    ```c
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```

Optimized:

a_i' = K_i

Second loop:  
\[
a_i = a_i' + a_{i-1}
\]
But careful: in second loop, `a[i]` is updated in place, and `prev` is the **new** `a[i]`.

\( \text{prev} = a_0 \)

\( \text{prev} = a_1 \)

\( a_2 = a_2^{(1)} + \text{prev} = K_2 + a_1 \)  
… which matches the original recurrence.

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

### **2. Semantic comparison**  

**Source code recurrence:**  
`a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]`  

**Optimized code recurrence:**  
`a[i] = prev + s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]`  
where `prev` starts as `a[0]` for `i = 1`, then `prev = a[i]` after computing `a[i]`.  

At first glance, this seems equivalent, but there’s a subtle issue:  
In the original loop, `a[i-1]` in iteration `i` is the value **from the previous iteration of the same loop** (not from a previous iteration of the outer `nl` loop).  
In the optimized version, `prev` is initialized to `a[0]` **at the start of each outer loop iteration (`nl`)**.  

But `a[0]` is never updated inside the inner loop. In the original code, `a[0]` is unchanged across all iterations.  
So for the first `nl` iteration, the results match.  

But for the second `nl` iteration, the original code uses `a[i-1]` from the **end of the previous inner loop** (since `a` was updated in the previous `nl` iteration).  
The optimized code resets `prev` to `a[0]` (unchanged) at the start of each `nl` iteration, which is wrong — it should start with `a[0]` from the **current state after previous `nl` iterations**, but `a[0]` is never changed, so maybe that’s fine? Wait — let’s check carefully.  

---

**Example:**  
Let `LEN_1D = 3`, `s1 = 1`, `s2 = 2`, `s_sum = 3`.  
Let `a = [10, 0, 0]`, `b = [100, 200, 300]`, `c = [1000, 2000, 3000]`, `d = [10000, 20000, 30000]`.  
Ignore outer loop for now (`nl = 0` only).  

**Original:**  
`i=1`: `a[1] = 10 + 3 + 200 + 2000 + 20000 = 22213`  
`i=2`: `a[2] = 22213 + 3 + 300 + 3000 + 30000 = 55516`  

**Optimized:**  
`prev = a[0] = 10`  
`i=1`: `a[1] = 10 + 3 + 200 + 2000 + 20000 = 22213`, `prev = 22213`  
`i=2`: `a[2] = 22213 + 3 + 300 + 3000 + 30000 = 55516`  

Matches for `nl=0`.  

Now `nl=1` (second outer iteration):  

**Original:**  
`i=1`: `a[1] = 10 + 3 + 200 + 2000 + 20000 = 22213` (same as before? Wait, `a[1]` was already 22213 from `nl=0`, but formula uses `a[0]` which is still 10, so it recomputes same value).  
`i=2`: `a[2] = 22213 + 3 + 300 + 3000 + 30000 = 55516` (same).  

So actually, because `a[i]` depends only on `a[i-1]` from the **same inner loop iteration**, and `a[i-1]` is recomputed in the same inner loop iteration before `a[i]`, the loop is **recomputing the same values each `nl` iteration** — no cumulative effect across `nl`.  

Thus resetting `prev = a[0]` each `nl` is fine, because `a[0]` never changes.  

So semantically, optimized matches original.  

---

### **3. Vectorization analysis**  

Compiler feedback says:  
> loop not vectorized: value that could not be identified as reduction is used outside the loop  

This is because `prev` is carried across iterations (true dependency), and after the loop, `prev` is not used except to compute `a[i]`. But the compiler cannot safely vectorize a recurrence like `a[i] = a[i-1] + ...` without special handling (like finding it’s a first-order linear recurrence).  

The optimized code still has the same recurrence pattern, so it’s not vectorizable automatically.  

---

### **4. Optimization opportunity**  

The recurrence is unavoidable here — each `a[i]` depends on `a[i-1]` directly.  
However, we could try to improve by:  
- Unrolling to expose some parallelism (but still limited).  
- Using SIMD for the independent parts (`b[i] + c[i] + d[i] + s_sum`), then doing a prefix sum sequentially.  

But the compiler’s remark indicates it tried and failed to auto-vectorize due to the recurrence.  

Given the constraints, the optimized code is already a minimal improvement (hoisting `s1 + s2`), but it’s **not vectorized** and the recurrence prevents easy vectorization.  

Since there’s an optimization opportunity (could manually vectorize via segmented/parallel prefix sums for large LEN_1D), this is a **FAIL** based on rule 3: “If there is optimization opportunity, output FAIL.”

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:

float s_sum = s1 + s2;
for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {

    for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

---

**1. Compilation errors in the optimized code**  
The optimized code uses `__builtin_alloca` inside the loop, which is valid in GCC/Clang for stack allocation. However, allocating inside the loop repeatedly is inefficient and could cause stack overflow if `LEN_1D` is large. But there’s no *syntax* error, so it compiles.

---

**2. Semantic comparison**  

The original recurrence:  
`a[i] = a[i-1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]` for `i = 1..LEN_1D-1`.

The optimized version splits into:  
1. `temp[i-1] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]`  
2. `a[i] = prev + temp[i-1]; prev = a[i]`.

Mathematically, this is equivalent to:  
`a[i] = a[i-1] + (s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i])`,  
which matches the source because `s_sum = s1 + s2`.

But let’s test with a small example:

**Example 1:**  
`LEN_1D = 3`, `s1=1`, `s2=2`, `a=[10,0,0]`, `b=[0,3,0]`, `c=[0,4,0]`, `d=[0,5,0]`.

Original:  
i=1: `a[1] = 10 + 1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 5 = 25`  
i=2: `a[2] = 25 + 1 + 2 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 28`

Optimized:  
`s_sum=3`  
temp[0] = 3 + 3 + 4 + 5 = 15  
temp[1] = 3 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 3  
prev = a[0] = 10  
i=1: `a[1] = 10 + 15 = 25`, prev=25  
i=2: `a[2] = 25 + 3 = 28` ✅ matches.

**Example 2:**  
`LEN_1D=4`, `a=[5,0,0,0]`, `b=[0,1,2,3]`, `c=0`, `d=0`, `s1=0`, `s2=0`.

Original:  
i=1: `a[1] = 5 + 0 + 0 + 1 + 0 + 0 = 6`  
i=2: `a[2] = 6 + 0 + 0 + 2 + 0 + 0 = 8`  
i=3: `a[3] = 8 + 0 + 0 + 3 + 0 + 0 = 11`

Optimized:  
`s_sum=0`  
temp[0]=1, temp[1]=2, temp[2]=3  
prev=5  
i=1: `a[1]=5+1=6`, prev=6  
i=2: `a[2]=6+2=8`, prev=8  
i=3: `a[3]=8+3=11` ✅ matches.

**Example 3:**  
`LEN_1D=2`, `a=[100,200]`, `b=[0,7]`, `c=[0,8]`, `d=[0,9]`, `s1=1`, `s2=2`.

Original:  
i=1: `a[1] = 100 + 1 + 2 + 7 + 8 + 9 = 127` (overwrites original 200)

Optimized:  
temp[0] = 3 + 7 + 8 + 9 = 27  
prev = 100  
i=1: `a[1] = 100 + 27 = 127` ✅ matches.

Semantics are consistent.

---

**3. Vectorization analysis**  

Compiler feedback says:  
- First loop (computing temp) is vectorized (width 4, interleaved 4).  
- Second loop (recurrence) is **not** vectorized because of a loop-carried dependency (`prev` depends on previous iteration).

The recurrence `a[i] = a[i-1] + ...` is inherently sequential; cannot be vectorized directly. The optimization attempted to separate independent parts, but the core recurrence remains.

However, the compiler’s remark is about the second loop in the optimized code, which is expected not to vectorize. The unit test analysis says “semantics are inconsistent” — but our manual check shows they are consistent. Possibly the unit test analysis was mistaken or used a different initial condition.

Given the recurrence is sequential, vectorization of the main update is impossible without changing the algorithm (e.g., using parallel prefix sums if the added term were independent of `i`, but here `b[i]+c[i]+d[i]` varies per `i`, so prefix sum still needed).

---

**4. Optimization opportunity**  

The optimized code allocates `temp` inside the `nl` loop — this is wasteful and could hurt performance due to repeated alloca. Better to allocate once outside the loops or reuse a static buffer. Also, splitting into two loops adds extra memory traffic (store/load of temp), which may not be beneficial unless `LEN_1D` is huge and `b,c,d` are not already in cache.

Given the recurrence cannot be vectorized, the only possible improvement is reducing overhead inside the loop:  
- Hoist `s_sum` calculation outside loops (already done).  
- Fuse back into one loop to avoid extra memory temp array.  

But the original loop is already minimal:  
`a[i] = a[i-1] + s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]`  
No extra temp needed.

Thus, the “optimized” version is actually worse due to extra memory usage and extra passes.

---

**Conclusion:**  
The optimized code is semantically correct but introduces unnecessary temporary storage and extra loop passes, which is not an optimization. The recurrence prevents vectorization, so the original loop is already optimal for serial execution. The compiler feedback shows the second loop not vectorized — which is expected and unavoidable.

Given the unit test says semantics are inconsistent (though our check shows they are consistent), there might be a subtle bug in the optimized version if `LEN_1D` changes between outer loop iterations (it doesn’t) or if `a[0]` changes in outer loop (it doesn’t in the given code, but if `a[0]` were updated inside the outer loop before the inner loop, the optimized version uses initial `a[0]` from start each time — but original does too because `a[0]` is never written in inner loop). Actually, in original, `a[0]` is only read at i=1, not updated. In optimized, `prev = a[0]` at start of each inner loop — same.

Wait — in original, `a[i]` depends on `a[i-1]` from *same* iteration of outer loop, but in optimized, `prev = a[0]` is taken at start of inner loop each outer iteration. That’s fine because `a[0]` is unchanged in inner loop. But what if `a[0]` is changed in outer loop before inner loop? That’s fine — both versions use updated `a[0]`.

Actually, the unit test’s “semantics are inconsistent” might be because in the optimized version, `temp[i-1]` uses `b[i]`, `c[i]`, `d[i]` from the *current* outer iteration — same as original. So no issue.

Given all this, the optimized code is correct but not better. The compiler’s non-vectorization of second loop is expected. The only “FAIL” reason is that the optimization introduced extra memory overhead without enabling vectorization of the recurrence, so it’s not an improvement.

   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

void s242_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float s1, float s2, float* a, float* b, float* c, float* d) {
    float s_sum = s1 + s2;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/5; nl++) {

        float a0_original = a[0];
        
        // Precompute independent sums for all i
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {

        // Sequential recurrence using precomputed values
        float prev = a0_original;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
            a[i] = prev + a[i];
            prev = a[i];
        }
    }
}

**2. Semantic comparison**  

The original loop:  
```c
for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; ++i) {
    a[i] = a[i - 1] + s1 + s2 + b[i] + c[i] + d[i];
}
```
This is a **recurrence**: `a[i]` depends on `a[i-1]`, so it’s sequential.  

The optimized code splits into two loops:  
- First loop: `a[i] = s_sum + b[i] + c[i] + d[i]` (independent of `a[i-1]`)  
- Second loop: `a[i] = prev + a[i]` where `prev` starts as `a[0]` and updates each iteration.  

Let’s test with a small example:  

**Example 1:**  
`LEN_1D = 3`, `a = [10, 20, 30]`, `b = [0, 1, 2]`, `c = [0, 0, 0]`, `d = [0, 0, 0]`, `s1 = 1`, `s2 = 2`.

First loop:  
i=1: `a[1] = 3 + 1 + 0 + 0 = 4`  
i=2: `a[2] = 3 + 2 + 0 + 0 = 5`  
Second loop:  
prev = a[0] = 10  
i=1: `a[1] = 10 + 4 = 14`, prev = 14  
i=2: `a[2] = 14 + 5 = 19`  
Result: `[10, 14, 19]` ✅ matches.  

**Example 2:**

Second loop:  
prev = 5  
i=1: `a[1] = 5 + 10 = 15`, prev=15  
i=2: `a[2] = 15 + 20 = 35`, prev=35  
i=3: `a[3] = 35 + 30 = 65` ✅ matches.

So the transformation is **mathematically correct** for a single iteration of the outer loop.  

But note: the outer loop runs `iterations/5` times, and `a` changes each outer iteration, so the next outer iteration uses the updated `a` from the previous outer iteration.

**3. Vectorization analysis**  

Compiler feedback says:  
- First inner loop (precomputation) is vectorized.  
- Second inner loop (recurrence) is **not vectorized** due to loop-carried dependency (prev update).  

That’s expected: the recurrence `a[i] = a[i-1] + ...` is inherently sequential.  

But the original loop also has that dependency, so it wouldn’t vectorize either. The optimized version splits the loop so that the first part can be vectorized, but the second part still can’t. This is **not worse** than the original in terms of vectorization; it’s actually better because part of the work is vectorized.  

However, the **total work** is increased: two loops over the same length instead of one, but the first loop is vectorized, so it might still be faster if the vectorized part outweighs the cost of the extra loop.  

---

**4. Optimization opportunity**  

The optimized code already improves vectorization for the independent part. The recurrence part cannot be vectorized without changing the algorithm (e.g., using parallel prefix sums if the operation is associative, but here it’s just addition, which is associative, so theoretically you could use a parallel prefix approach, but that’s a bigger change).  

Given the constraints, the optimized code is already doing a good job:  
- It vectorizes the independent sum computation.  
- It keeps the recurrence sequential (must be).  

No further simple modification is needed for vectorization; the recurrence is the bottleneck.  

---

**5. Conclusion**  

The semantics are correct, the code compiles, and part of it is vectorized. The recurrence part cannot be vectorized, which is inherent to the original algorithm.  

Thus, the optimized code is **fully optimized** under the given constraints.
